# M4 AH Full Copy Bank v2.0

Reconciled 6 October 2026. 182 daily themes; up to 546 units. Launch-day schedule with indicative dates. First 28 daily packages are bespoke rewritten copy; later packages are development drafts for rolling four-week editing. This replaces v1.0; it does not certify rendered assets, current prices or live destinations.

VISIBLE COPY and VISIBLE LABEL are the only rendered text. ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY contains no extra wording to paste onto the slide. Every simulated slide carries the illustrative label except the three-word cover, where disclosure is carried by caption/profile. Final design must preserve visible disclosures.

## Launch day 1 · Week 1, day 1 · Busy Isn’t Booked

Indicative date: 2026-10-12; not a committed launch date.
Theme: Meet Keisha: fully booked, not fully in control. Audience stage: Problem aware. Service fit: AI Receptionist / Website.
Status: Bespoke first-month copy; production and destination checks pending.

ARGUMENT / TURN: So you can see whether to attract enquiries or handle existing ones better. The diary is a clue, not the whole diagnosis.

COVERS: 1. Busy Isn’t Booked; 2. Tuesday Needs Answers; 3. Saturday Hides Gaps; 4. Count Empty Hours; 5. Diary Meets Inbox; 6. Full Day Myth; 7. Find Tuesday’s Gap; 8. Bookings Need Context

### Carousel

SLIDE 1 · OPEN · SHORT
VISIBLE COPY: Busy Isn’t Booked
VISIBLE LABEL: none; character disclosure in caption/profile
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Hero prop: Weekly diary beside enquiry statuses; cover words only

SLIDE 2 · HOLD · DENSE
VISIBLE COPY: Keisha’s Saturday is full. Tuesday still has an empty chair. A busy day cannot answer whether the whole week is working.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Scene establishing a full saturday hides an empty tuesday

SLIDE 3 · SPINE 1 · SHORT
VISIBLE COPY: Her diary shows two different things: booked appointments and hours still available. Count both before deciding she needs more attention.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Annotated artifact showing the missing detail: Weekly diary beside enquiry statuses

SLIDE 4 · SPINE 2 · DENSE
VISIBLE COPY: Now add enquiries. Which received a reply, which booked, and which remain unanswered? The empty chair may begin in the inbox.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Consequence diagram using only the visible copy

SLIDE 5 · SPINE 3 · SHORT
VISIBLE COPY: Mark each enquiry: waiting for reply, waiting for client, booked or closed. A status is more useful than another unread badge.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Revised artifact: Compare booked hours with available hours by day

SLIDE 6 · SPINE 4 · DENSE
VISIBLE COPY: Compare one week’s available hours with attended appointments and unresolved enquiries. Keep those measures separate; they describe different parts of the journey.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Test card or controlled interface view for: Count the unresolved enquiries beside a sample weekly diary

SLIDE 7 · TURN · SHORT
VISIBLE COPY: So you can see whether to attract enquiries or handle existing ones better. The diary is a clue, not the whole diagnosis.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Espresso statement board; emphasise the reframed belief

SLIDE 8 · PAYOFF · DENSE
VISIBLE COPY: A full Saturday does not explain Tuesday. Check one week of enquiries against your diary.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example

Theme: Meet Keisha: fully booked, not fully in control. Audience stage: Problem aware. Service fit: AI Receptionist / Website.

ARGUMENT / TURN: So you can turn a price conversation into a booking decision. The missing piece was clarity, not another discount.

COVERS: 1. DM Limbo; 2. Price Isn’t Clarity; 3. Hair Included?; 4. Answer The Inclusion; 5. Quote Still Unfinished; 6. Unanswered Booking Question; 7. Clarify Before Discounting; 8. Price Needs Context

## Launch day 5 · Week 1, day 5 · Hidden Praise

Indicative date: 2026-10-16; not a committed launch date.
Theme: Meet Keisha: fully booked, not fully in control. Audience stage: Problem aware. Service fit: AI Receptionist / Website.
Status: Bespoke first-month copy; production and destination checks pending.

ARGUMENT / TURN: So you can make real experience visible without turning private praise into assumed consent. Trust needs a sound route to publication.

COVERS: 1. Hidden Praise; 2. Private Isn’t Public; 3. Permission Before Proof; 4. Praise Needs Permission; 5. Honest Reviews Only; 6. Make Experience Visible; 7. Thank-You Isn’t Consent; 8. Trust Needs Context

### Carousel

SLIDE 1 · OPEN · SHORT
VISIBLE COPY: Hidden Praise
VISIBLE LABEL: none; character disclosure in caption/profile
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Hero prop: Private-message card separated from review-link card; cover words only

VISIBLE COPY: A client messages, “Thank you, I love it.” Keisha has private praise. A new visitor cannot see that experience.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Scene establishing a client thanks the salon privately

SLIDE 3 · SPINE 1 · SHORT
VISIBLE COPY: Copying the message into an advert would change its audience. A private thank-you is not automatically permission to publish.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Annotated artifact showing the missing detail: Private-message card separated from review-link card

SLIDE 4 · SPINE 2 · DENSE

VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Consequence diagram using only the visible copy

SLIDE 5 · SPINE 3 · SHORT
VISIBLE COPY: Use a neutral review invitation for all clients, regardless of satisfaction. Keep testimonial permission as a separate recorded decision.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Revised artifact: Offer every client the same optional honest review link

SLIDE 6 · SPINE 4 · DENSE

ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Test card or controlled interface view for: Test a review QR card without submitting a review

VISIBLE COPY: So you can make real experience visible without turning private praise into assumed consent. Trust needs a sound route to publication.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Espresso statement board; emphasise the reframed belief

VISIBLE COPY: Private praise and public proof are different assets. Test your honest-review link.
VISIBLE LABEL: Illustrative example
ARTIFACT / DESIGN ONLY: Cream action card; show only the belief and single action
